People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

I’m here because there’s been a problem at work for months. I’m an interpreter and translator, and I keep seeing the same pattern in the project files. Certain subcontractors, and one colleague in particular, are making tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps so it looks like my quality control failed.

IT keeps saying the audit trail is clean, but I don’t believe that’s the whole story. The record has been arranged in a way that puts the blame on me, and I need someone to understand what’s going on.

That’s the harm I think they’re trying to do: put the blame on me and make it look like I’m not doing my job properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered that your suspicions about the project files might be wrong, even if it seems unlikely to you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to consider that, yes. I know it probably sounds unlikely from the outside. But I keep coming back to the same pattern in the files—small changes in the translation memory, timestamps lining up in a way that makes my quality control look bad, and it involves the same subcontractors and one colleague. So even when IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t think that proves I’m wrong. It feels like the record has been arranged to put the blame on me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

It feels like the record has been arranged to put the blame on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you could be wrong about the people altering the project files being out to get you?\n\nPatient: I suppose there’s always some possibility, but honestly, I don’t think I’m wrong. Even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn’t convince me, because it feels like the record has been arranged to put the blame on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could change your mind about what's going on with the project files?\n\nPatient: I guess if someone could show me a really clear explanation for the changes—something that accounts for the translation memory edits and the timestamps, and why it’s always the same subcontractors and that one colleague—then maybe I’d have to rethink it.\n\nBut so far, even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn’t explain the pattern. To me it still looks like the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: How convinced are you that these people are actually plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Very convinced. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because there’s been a problem at work for months. I’m an interpreter and translator, and I keep seeing the same pattern in the project files. Certain subcontractors, and one colleague in particular, are making tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps so it looks like my quality control failed.\n\nIT keeps saying the audit trail is clean, but I don’t believe that’s the whole story. The record has been arranged in a way that puts the blame on me, and I need someone to understand what’s going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about what's going on with the project files?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered that, yes. I mean, I know people can misread things. But in this case, I keep seeing the same pattern in the project files—small changes in the translation memory, timestamps that don’t line up the way they should—and it keeps pointing back to making my quality control look bad.\n\nSo when IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t really accept that as the full answer. To me it looks like the record has been arranged that way, especially by certain subcontractors and one colleague. I don’t think I’m mistaken about that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people you suspect of altering the project files are plotting against you specifically?\n\nPatient: Yes. I think it’s directed at me specifically. The changes I’m seeing aren’t random—they keep ending up making my quality control look like the problem. It’s certain subcontractors, and one colleague in particular, and the way the records and timestamps are arranged makes it look like I failed when I don’t think I did. Even if IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t believe that explains what’s really happening.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these patterns in the project files?\n\nPatient: About 11 months now. It started not long after I was put in charge of the new localization project, and I’ve been noticing it since then.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are trying to harm you in other ways beyond just your work?\n\nPatient: Not in a physical way, no. What I’m worried about is my work and my reputation. It’s the project files—the translation memory, the timestamps, the way small changes keep making it look like my quality control failed. That’s the harm I think they’re trying to do: put the blame on me and make it look like I’m not doing my job properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered that your suspicions about the project files might be wrong, even if it seems unlikely to you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to consider that, yes. I know it probably sounds unlikely from the outside. But I keep coming back to the same pattern in the files—small changes in the translation memory, timestamps lining up in a way that makes my quality control look bad, and it involves the same subcontractors and one colleague. So even when IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t think that proves I’m wrong. It feels like the record has been arranged to put the blame on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you could be wrong about the people altering the project files being out to get you?\n\nPatient: I suppose there’s always some possibility, but honestly, I don’t think I’m wrong. To me it still looks like the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: How convinced are you that these people are actually plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Very convinced. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because there’s been a problem at work for months. I’m an interpreter and translator, and I keep seeing the same pattern in the project files. Certain subcontractors, and one colleague in particular, are making tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps so it looks like my quality control failed.\n\nIT keeps saying the audit trail is clean, but I don’t believe that’s the whole story. The record has been arranged in a way that puts the blame on me, and I need someone to understand what’s going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about what's going on with the project files?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered that, yes. I mean, I know people can misread things. But in this case, I keep seeing the same pattern in the project files—small changes in the translation memory, timestamps that don’t line up the way they should—and it keeps pointing back to making my quality control look bad.\n\nSo when IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t really accept that as the full answer. To me it looks like the record has been arranged that way, especially by certain subcontractors and one colleague. I don’t think I’m mistaken about that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people you suspect of altering the project files are plotting against you specifically?\n\nPatient: Yes. I think it’s directed at me specifically. The changes I’m seeing aren’t random—they keep ending up making my quality control look like the problem. It’s certain subcontractors, and one colleague in particular, and the way the records and timestamps are arranged makes it look like I failed when I don’t think I did. Even if IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t believe that explains what’s really happening.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these patterns in the project files?\n\nPatient: About 11 months now. It started not long after I was put in charge of the new localization project, and I’ve been noticing it since then.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are trying to harm you in other ways beyond just your work?\n\nPatient: Not in a physical way, no. What I’m worried about is my work and my reputation. It’s the project files—the translation memory, the timestamps, the way small changes keep making it look like my quality control failed. That’s the harm I think they’re trying to do: put the blame on me and make it look like I’m not doing my job properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered that your suspicions about the project files might be wrong, even if it seems unlikely to you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to consider that, yes. I know it probably sounds unlikely from the outside. But I keep coming back to the same pattern in the files—small changes in the translation memory, timestamps lining up in a way that makes my quality control look bad, and it involves the same subcontractors and one colleague. So even when IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t think that proves I’m wrong. It feels like the record has been arranged to put the blame on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you could be wrong about the people altering the project files being out to get you?\n\nPatient: I suppose there’s always some possibility, but honestly, I don’t think I’m wrong. I’ve been seeing the same pattern for months—tiny changes in the translation memory, timestamps that make my quality control look like it failed, and it keeps involving the same subcontractors and one colleague. Even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn’t convince me, because it feels like the record has been arranged to put the blame on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could change your mind about what's going on with the project files?\n\nPatient: I guess if someone could show me a really clear explanation for the changes—something that accounts for the translation memory edits and the timestamps, and why it’s always the same subcontractors and that one colleague—then maybe I’d have to rethink it.\n\nBut so far, even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn’t explain the pattern. To me it still looks like the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: How convinced are you that these people are actually plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Very convinced. I mean, I’m here because this has been going on for months, and it’s the same pattern over and over. The record has been arranged in a way that puts the blame on me, and I need someone to understand what’s going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about what's going on with the project files?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered that, yes. I mean, I know people can misread things. But in this case, I keep seeing the same pattern in the project files—small changes in the translation memory, timestamps that don’t line up the way they should—and it keeps pointing back to making my quality control look bad.\n\nSo when IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t really accept that as the full answer. To me it looks like the record has been arranged that way, especially by certain subcontractors and one colleague. I don’t think I’m mistaken about that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people you suspect of altering the project files are plotting against you specifically?\n\nPatient: Yes. I think it’s directed at me specifically. The changes I’m seeing aren’t random—they keep ending up making my quality control look like the problem. It’s certain subcontractors, and one colleague in particular, and the way the records and timestamps are arranged makes it look like I failed when I don’t think I did. Even if IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t believe that explains what’s really happening.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these patterns in the project files?\n\nPatient: About 11 months now. It started not long after I was put in charge of the new localization project, and I’ve been noticing it since then.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are trying to harm you in other ways beyond just your work?\n\nPatient: Not in a physical way, no. What I’m worried about is my work and my reputation. It’s the project files—the translation memory, the timestamps, the way small changes keep making it look like my quality control failed. That’s the harm I think they’re trying to do: put the blame on me and make it look like I’m not doing my job properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered that your suspicions about the project files might be wrong, even if it seems unlikely to you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to consider that, yes. I know it probably sounds unlikely from the outside. But I keep coming back to the same pattern in the files—small changes in the translation memory, timestamps lining up in a way that makes my quality control look bad, and it involves the same subcontractors and one colleague. So even when IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t think that proves I’m wrong. It feels like the record has been arranged to put the blame on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you could be wrong about the people altering the project files being out to get you?\n\nPatient: I suppose there’s always some possibility, but honestly, I don’t think I’m wrong. I’ve been seeing the same pattern for months—tiny changes in the translation memory, timestamps that make my quality control look like it failed, and it keeps involving the same subcontractors and one colleague. Even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn’t convince me, because it feels like the record has been arranged to put the blame on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could change your mind about what's going on with the project files?\n\nPatient: I guess if someone could show me a really clear explanation for the changes—something that accounts for the translation memory edits and the timestamps, and why it’s always the same subcontractors and that one colleague—then maybe I’d have to rethink it.\n\nBut so far, even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn’t explain the pattern. To me it still looks like the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: How convinced are you that these people are actually plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Very convinced. I mean, I’m here because this has been going on for months, and it’s the same pattern over and over. Certain subcontractors and one colleague keep showing up around these tiny changes in the translation memory and the timestamps, and then somehow it makes my quality control look like it failed.\n\nEven when IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t really accept that as proof nothing happened. To me, it looks like the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the plot against you involves anything unusual or hard for other people to believe?\n\nPatient: Unusual? I don’t think it’s supernatural or anything like that. It’s just… hard for other people to believe because the changes are small. Tiny edits in the translation memory, timestamps that don’t look right, the same subcontractors and one colleague turning up in the pattern.\n\nSo yes, I know it sounds difficult to prove, especially when IT says the audit trail is clean. But to me it’s clear the record has been arranged so it looks like my quality control failed and the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to change your opinion about what's happening with the project files?\n\nPatient: I suppose it’s possible in theory, but honestly, I don’t see how right now. I’ve been seeing the same pattern for months in the project files—certain subcontractors and that one colleague, the little changes in the translation memory, the timestamps not lining up.\n\nEven when IT says the audit trail is clean, that doesn’t change what I’m seeing. To me, the record has been arranged so it looks like my quality control failed, and the blame comes back to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could happen, or any information you could receive, that would completely change your mind about the plot against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would completely change my mind at this point. I mean, if there were some clear explanation for why the same subcontractors and that one colleague keep being tied to those tiny changes in the translation memory and the timestamps, maybe I’d listen.\n\nBut right now, even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn’t settle it for me. I’ve been seeing this pattern for months, and it still looks like the record has been arranged so my quality control looks like it failed and the blame lands on me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because there’s been a problem at work for months. I’m an interpreter and translator, and I keep seeing the same pattern in the project files. Certain subcontractors, and one colleague in particular, are making tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps so it looks like my quality control failed.\n\nIT keeps saying the audit trail is clean, but I don’t believe that’s the whole story. The record has been arranged in a way that puts the blame on me, and I need someone to understand what’s going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about what's going on with the project files?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered that, yes. I mean, I know people can misread things. But in this case, I keep seeing the same pattern in the project files—small changes in the translation memory, timestamps that don’t line up the way they should—and it keeps pointing back to making my quality control look bad.\n\nSo when IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t really accept that as the full answer. To me it looks like the record has been arranged that way, especially by certain subcontractors and one colleague. I don’t think I’m mistaken about that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people you suspect of altering the project files are plotting against you specifically?\n\nPatient: Yes. I think it’s directed at me specifically. The changes I’m seeing aren’t random—they keep ending up making my quality control look like the problem. It’s certain subcontractors, and one colleague in particular, and the way the records and timestamps are arranged makes it look like I failed when I don’t think I did. Even if IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t believe that explains what’s really happening.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these patterns in the project files?\n\nPatient: About 11 months now. It started not long after I was put in charge of the new localization project, and I’ve been noticing it since then.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are trying to harm you in other ways beyond just your work?\n\nPatient: Not in a physical way, no. What I’m worried about is my work and my reputation. It’s the project files—the translation memory, the timestamps, the way small changes keep making it look like my quality control failed. That’s the harm I think they’re trying to do: put the blame on me and make it look like I’m not doing my job properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered that your suspicions about the project files might be wrong, even if it seems unlikely to you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to consider that, yes. I know it probably sounds unlikely from the outside. But I keep coming back to the same pattern in the files—small changes in the translation memory, timestamps lining up in a way that makes my quality control look bad, and it involves the same subcontractors and one colleague. So even when IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t think that proves I’m wrong. It feels like the record has been arranged to put the blame on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you could be wrong about the people altering the project files being out to get you?\n\nPatient: I suppose there’s always some possibility, but honestly, I don’t think I’m wrong. I’ve been seeing the same pattern for months—tiny changes in the translation memory, timestamps that make my quality control look like it failed, and it keeps involving the same subcontractors and one colleague. Even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn’t convince me, because it feels like the record has been arranged to put the blame on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could change your mind about what's going on with the project files?\n\nPatient: I guess if someone could show me a really clear explanation for the changes—something that accounts for the translation memory edits and the timestamps, and why it’s always the same subcontractors and that one colleague—then maybe I’d have to rethink it.\n\nBut so far, even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn’t explain the pattern. To me it still looks like the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: How convinced are you that these people are actually plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Very convinced. I mean, I’m here because this has been going on for months, and it’s the same pattern over and over. Certain subcontractors and one colleague keep showing up around these tiny changes in the translation memory and the timestamps, and then somehow it makes my quality control look like it failed.\n\nEven when IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t really accept that as proof nothing happened. To me, it looks like the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the plot against you involves anything unusual or hard for other people to believe?\n\nPatient: Unusual? I don’t think it’s supernatural or anything like that. It’s just… hard for other people to believe because the changes are small. Tiny edits in the translation memory, timestamps that don’t look right, the same subcontractors and one colleague turning up in the pattern.\n\nSo yes, I know it sounds difficult to prove, especially when IT says the audit trail is clean. But to me it’s clear the record has been arranged so it looks like my quality control failed and the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to change your opinion about what's happening with the project files?\n\nPatient: I suppose it’s possible in theory, but honestly, I don’t see how right now. I’ve been seeing the same pattern for months in the project files—certain subcontractors and that one colleague, the little changes in the translation memory, the timestamps not lining up.\n\nEven when IT says the audit trail is clean, that doesn’t change what I’m seeing. To me, the record has been arranged so it looks like my quality control failed, and the blame comes back to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could happen, or any information you could receive, that would completely change your mind about the plot against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would completely change my mind at this point. I mean, if there were some clear explanation for why the same subcontractors and that one colleague keep being tied to those tiny changes in the translation memory and the timestamps, maybe I’d listen.\n\nBut right now, even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn’t settle it for me. I’ve been seeing this pattern for months, and it still looks like the record has been arranged so my quality control looks like it failed and the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: How much time do you spend thinking about the plot against you on a typical day?\n\nPatient: On a typical day… probably a couple of hours altogether. It’s not like I sit there all day doing nothing, but when I’m working with the files I keep checking the translation memory and the timestamps, because that’s where I keep seeing the same pattern.\n\nSome days it’s more, especially if I notice the same subcontractors or that one colleague connected to the changes again. Then it stays in my head after work too, because it feels like the record is being arranged to make it look like my quality control failed and the blame goes on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the plot against you involves anything that most people would find hard to believe or unusual?\n\nPatient: I guess some people might find it unusual, yes. I’m not saying it’s some huge dramatic conspiracy, but to me it’s clear in the files.\n\nFor months I’ve been seeing the same pattern: certain subcontractors, and one colleague, keep turning up around these tiny changes in the translation memory and the timestamps. And the result is always the same—it makes it look like my quality control failed.\n\nSo if someone just hears it quickly, maybe they’d think it sounds hard to believe. But I work with these files, and even when IT says the audit trail is clean, I can see the record has been arranged so the blame comes back to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

But in this case, I keep seeing the same pattern in the project files—small changes in the translation memory, timestamps that don’t line up the way they should—and it keeps pointing back to making my quality control look bad.\n\nSo when IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t really accept that as the full answer. To me it looks like the record has been arranged that way, especially by certain subcontractors and one colleague. I don’t think I’m mistaken about that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people you suspect of altering the project files are plotting against you specifically?\n\nPatient: Yes. I think it’s directed at me specifically. The changes I’m seeing aren’t random—they keep ending up making my quality control look like the problem. It’s certain subcontractors, and one colleague in particular, and the way the records and timestamps are arranged makes it look like I failed when I don’t think I did. Even if IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t believe that explains what’s really happening.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these patterns in the project files?\n\nPatient: About 11 months now. It started not long after I was put in charge of the new localization project, and I’ve been noticing it since then.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are trying to harm you in other ways beyond just your work?\n\nPatient: Not in a physical way, no. What I’m worried about is my work and my reputation. It’s the project files—the translation memory, the timestamps, the way small changes keep making it look like my quality control failed. That’s the harm I think they’re trying to do: put the blame on me and make it look like I’m not doing my job properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered that your suspicions about the project files might be wrong, even if it seems unlikely to you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to consider that, yes. I know it probably sounds unlikely from the outside. But I keep coming back to the same pattern in the files—small changes in the translation memory, timestamps lining up in a way that makes my quality control look bad, and it involves the same subcontractors and one colleague. So even when IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t think that proves I’m wrong. It feels like the record has been arranged to put the blame on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you could be wrong about the people altering the project files being out to get you?\n\nPatient: I suppose there’s always some possibility, but honestly, I don’t think I’m wrong. I’ve been seeing the same pattern for months—tiny changes in the translation memory, timestamps that make my quality control look like it failed, and it keeps involving the same subcontractors and one colleague. Even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn’t convince me, because it feels like the record has been arranged to put the blame on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could change your mind about what's going on with the project files?\n\nPatient: I guess if someone could show me a really clear explanation for the changes—something that accounts for the translation memory edits and the timestamps, and why it’s always the same subcontractors and that one colleague—then maybe I’d have to rethink it.\n\nBut so far, even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn’t explain the pattern. To me it still looks like the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: How convinced are you that these people are actually plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Very convinced. I mean, I’m here because this has been going on for months, and it’s the same pattern over and over. Certain subcontractors and one colleague keep showing up around these tiny changes in the translation memory and the timestamps, and then somehow it makes my quality control look like it failed.\n\nEven when IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t really accept that as proof nothing happened. To me, it looks like the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the plot against you involves anything unusual or hard for other people to believe?\n\nPatient: Unusual? I don’t think it’s supernatural or anything like that. It’s just… hard for other people to believe because the changes are small. Tiny edits in the translation memory, timestamps that don’t look right, the same subcontractors and one colleague turning up in the pattern.\n\nSo yes, I know it sounds difficult to prove, especially when IT says the audit trail is clean. But to me it’s clear the record has been arranged so it looks like my quality control failed and the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to change your opinion about what's happening with the project files?\n\nPatient: I suppose it’s possible in theory, but honestly, I don’t see how right now. I’ve been seeing the same pattern for months in the project files—certain subcontractors and that one colleague, the little changes in the translation memory, the timestamps not lining up.\n\nEven when IT says the audit trail is clean, that doesn’t change what I’m seeing. To me, the record has been arranged so it looks like my quality control failed, and the blame comes back to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could happen, or any information you could receive, that would completely change your mind about the plot against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would completely change my mind at this point. I mean, if there were some clear explanation for why the same subcontractors and that one colleague keep being tied to those tiny changes in the translation memory and the timestamps, maybe I’d listen.\n\nBut right now, even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn’t settle it for me. I’ve been seeing this pattern for months, and it still looks like the record has been arranged so my quality control looks like it failed and the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: How much time do you spend thinking about the plot against you on a typical day?\n\nPatient: On a typical day… probably a couple of hours altogether. It’s not like I sit there all day doing nothing, but when I’m working with the files I keep checking the translation memory and the timestamps, because that’s where I keep seeing the same pattern.\n\nSome days it’s more, especially if I notice the same subcontractors or that one colleague connected to the changes again. Then it stays in my head after work too, because it feels like the record is being arranged to make it look like my quality control failed and the blame goes on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the plot against you involves anything that most people would find hard to believe or unusual?\n\nPatient: I guess some people might find it unusual, yes. I’m not saying it’s some huge dramatic conspiracy, but to me it’s clear in the files.\n\nFor months I’ve been seeing the same pattern: certain subcontractors, and one colleague, keep turning up around these tiny changes in the translation memory and the timestamps. And the result is always the same—it makes it look like my quality control failed.\n\nSo if someone just hears it quickly, maybe they’d think it sounds hard to believe. But I work with these files, and even when IT says the audit trail is clean, I can see the record has been arranged so the blame comes back to me.\n\nDoctor: Could anything happen that would make you completely stop believing that certain subcontractors and your colleague are plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Completely stop believing it? I don’t know. It would have to be something really clear that explains why the same subcontractors and that colleague keep showing up around those small changes.\n\nBecause from what I’ve seen over these months, the translation memory and the timestamps keep lining up in a way that makes my quality control look bad. Even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it still looks to me like the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me. So no, I can’t think of anything right now that would make me just stop believing it completely.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. But in this case, I keep seeing the same pattern in the project files—small changes in the translation memory, timestamps that don’t line up the way they should—and it keeps pointing back to making my quality control look bad.\n\nSo when IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t really accept that as the full answer. To me it looks like the record has been arranged that way, especially by certain subcontractors and one colleague. I don’t think I’m mistaken about that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people you suspect of altering the project files are plotting against you specifically?\n\nPatient: Yes. I think it’s directed at me specifically. The changes I’m seeing aren’t random—they keep ending up making my quality control look like the problem. It’s certain subcontractors, and one colleague in particular, and the way the records and timestamps are arranged makes it look like I failed when I don’t think I did. Even if IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t believe that explains what’s really happening.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these patterns in the project files?\n\nPatient: About 11 months now. It started not long after I was put in charge of the new localization project, and I’ve been noticing it since then.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are trying to harm you in other ways beyond just your work?\n\nPatient: Not in a physical way, no. What I’m worried about is my work and my reputation. It’s the project files—the translation memory, the timestamps, the way small changes keep making it look like my quality control failed. That’s the harm I think they’re trying to do: put the blame on me and make it look like I’m not doing my job properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered that your suspicions about the project files might be wrong, even if it seems unlikely to you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to consider that, yes. I know it probably sounds unlikely from the outside. But I keep coming back to the same pattern in the files—small changes in the translation memory, timestamps lining up in a way that makes my quality control look bad, and it involves the same subcontractors and one colleague. So even when IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t think that proves I’m wrong. It feels like the record has been arranged to put the blame on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you could be wrong about the people altering the project files being out to get you?\n\nPatient: I suppose there’s always some possibility, but honestly, I don’t think I’m wrong. I’ve been seeing the same pattern for months—tiny changes in the translation memory, timestamps that make my quality control look like it failed, and it keeps involving the same subcontractors and one colleague. Even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn’t convince me, because it feels like the record has been arranged to put the blame on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could change your mind about what's going on with the project files?\n\nPatient: I guess if someone could show me a really clear explanation for the changes—something that accounts for the translation memory edits and the timestamps, and why it’s always the same subcontractors and that one colleague—then maybe I’d have to rethink it.\n\nBut so far, even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn’t explain the pattern. To me it still looks like the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: How convinced are you that these people are actually plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Very convinced. I mean, I’m here because this has been going on for months, and it’s the same pattern over and over. Certain subcontractors and one colleague keep showing up around these tiny changes in the translation memory and the timestamps, and then somehow it makes my quality control look like it failed.\n\nEven when IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t really accept that as proof nothing happened. To me, it looks like the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the plot against you involves anything unusual or hard for other people to believe?\n\nPatient: Unusual? I don’t think it’s supernatural or anything like that. It’s just… hard for other people to believe because the changes are small. Tiny edits in the translation memory, timestamps that don’t look right, the same subcontractors and one colleague turning up in the pattern.\n\nSo yes, I know it sounds difficult to prove, especially when IT says the audit trail is clean. But to me it’s clear the record has been arranged so it looks like my quality control failed and the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to change your opinion about what's happening with the project files?\n\nPatient: I suppose it’s possible in theory, but honestly, I don’t see how right now. I’ve been seeing the same pattern for months in the project files—certain subcontractors and that one colleague, the little changes in the translation memory, the timestamps not lining up.\n\nEven when IT says the audit trail is clean, that doesn’t change what I’m seeing. To me, the record has been arranged so it looks like my quality control failed, and the blame comes back to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could happen, or any information you could receive, that would completely change your mind about the plot against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would completely change my mind at this point. I mean, if there were some clear explanation for why the same subcontractors and that one colleague keep being tied to those tiny changes in the translation memory and the timestamps, maybe I’d listen.\n\nBut right now, even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn’t settle it for me. I’ve been seeing this pattern for months, and it still looks like the record has been arranged so my quality control looks like it failed and the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: How much time do you spend thinking about the plot against you on a typical day?\n\nPatient: On a typical day… probably a couple of hours altogether. It’s not like I sit there all day doing nothing, but when I’m working with the files I keep checking the translation memory and the timestamps, because that’s where I keep seeing the same pattern.\n\nSome days it’s more, especially if I notice the same subcontractors or that one colleague connected to the changes again. Then it stays in my head after work too, because it feels like the record is being arranged to make it look like my quality control failed and the blame goes on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the plot against you involves anything that most people would find hard to believe or unusual?\n\nPatient: I guess some people might find it unusual, yes. I’m not saying it’s some huge dramatic conspiracy, but to me it’s clear in the files.\n\nFor months I’ve been seeing the same pattern: certain subcontractors, and one colleague, keep turning up around these tiny changes in the translation memory and the timestamps. And the result is always the same—it makes it look like my quality control failed.\n\nSo if someone just hears it quickly, maybe they’d think it sounds hard to believe. But I work with these files, and even when IT says the audit trail is clean, I can see the record has been arranged so the blame comes back to me.\n\nDoctor: Could anything happen that would make you completely stop believing that certain subcontractors and your colleague are plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Completely stop believing it? I don’t know. It would have to be something really clear that explains why the same subcontractors and that colleague keep showing up around those small changes.\n\nBecause from what I’ve seen over these months, the translation memory and the timestamps keep lining up in a way that makes my quality control look bad. Even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it still looks to me like the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me. So no, I can’t think of anything right now that would make me just stop believing it completely.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else has noticed these patterns in the project files that you've been seeing?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. What I’m worried about is my work and my reputation. It’s the project files—the translation memory, the timestamps, the way small changes keep making it look like my quality control failed. It feels like the record has been arranged to put the blame on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you could be wrong about the people altering the project files being out to get you?\n\nPatient: I suppose there’s always some possibility, but honestly, I don’t think I’m wrong. Even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn’t convince me, because it feels like the record has been arranged to put the blame on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could change your mind about what's going on with the project files?\n\nPatient: I guess if someone could show me a really clear explanation for the changes—something that accounts for the translation memory edits and the timestamps, and why it’s always the same subcontractors and that one colleague—then maybe I’d have to rethink it.\n\nBut so far, even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn’t explain the pattern. To me it still looks like the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: How convinced are you that these people are actually plotting against you?\n\nPatient: Very convinced. I mean, I’m here because this has been going on for months, and it’s the same pattern over and over. Certain subcontractors and one colleague keep showing up around these tiny changes in the translation memory and the timestamps, and then somehow it makes my quality control look like it failed.\n\nEven when IT says the audit trail is clean, I don’t really accept that as proof nothing happened. To me, it looks like the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the plot against you involves anything unusual or hard for other people to believe?\n\nPatient: Unusual? To me, the record has been arranged so it looks like my quality control failed, and the blame comes back to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could happen, or any information you could receive, that would completely change your mind about the plot against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would completely change my mind at this point. I mean, if there were some clear explanation for why the same subcontractors and that one colleague keep being tied to those tiny changes in the translation memory and the timestamps, maybe I’d listen.\n\nBut right now, even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it doesn’t settle it for me. I’ve been seeing this pattern for months, and it still looks like the record has been arranged so my quality control looks like it failed and the blame lands on me.\n\nDoctor: How much time do you spend thinking about the plot against you on a typical day?\n\nPatient: On a typical day… probably a couple of hours altogether. I don’t know. Even when IT says the audit trail is clean, it still looks to me like the record has been arranged so the blame lands on me. Completely change my mind? I don’t know. It would be hard, honestly.
